(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s051/reader_a_pilot.md)

# s051 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. P1 (51:1-23): Directed Provision
- Semantic invariant: Sustenance becomes available through directed movement, containment, release, cultivation, and measured transfer.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:1-4 wind, bearing, running, and division; 51:15 springs and gardens; 51:19 an acknowledged claim in wealth; 51:22 provision in the sky.
- Surprising reach: The opening motions resolve into one supply system: atmospheric freight becomes routed water, productive land, and finally an apportioned human share.

#### Subchannel A. Atmospheric Freight
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Wind sorts and transports a heavy, rain-bearing load until what is held aloft falls as provision.
- Active motifs: winnowing scatter (`quranic:root_000513:B004/m01`); visible load-bearing (`quranic:root_000357:B001/m01`); heavy freight (`quranic:root_001674:B002/m01`); rain-bearing cloud (`quranic:root_000357:B010/m01`); cloud and rain (`quranic:root_000745:B004/m02`); falling rain (`quranic:root_001675:B003/m01`); rain as provision (`quranic:root_000560:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:1 `ذَّٰرِيَٰتِ/ذَرْوًا` (ذ ر و); 51:2 `حَٰمِلَٰتِ` (ح م ل), `وِقْرًا` (و ق ر); 51:6 `وَٰقِعٌ` (و ق ع); 51:7,22 `سَّمَآءِ` (س م و); 51:22 `رِزْقُ` (ر ز ق).
- Synthesis: Scattering is not mere dispersal. It separates and mobilizes a burden whose latent identity is a rain cloud; the burden's eventual falling turns transport into delivery. The sky's promised provision is thereby materialized as freight moving through an atmospheric distribution chain.

#### Subchannel B. Routed Water
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Released water enters channels, arrives from elsewhere, and gathers in springs before continuing through the landscape.
- Active motifs: flowing water (`quranic:root_000240:B001/m01`); watercourse or channel (`quranic:root_000009:B004/m01`); incoming flood from another land (`quranic:root_000009:B005/m01`); spring or water source (`quranic:root_001069:B006/m01`); rainfall as provision (`quranic:root_000560:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:3 `جَٰرِيَٰتِ` (ج ر ي); 51:15 `عُيُونٍ` (ع ي ن); 51:16 `ءَاتَىٰ` (ء ت ي); 51:22 `رِزْقُ` (ر ز ق).
- Synthesis: The running motion of 51:3 acquires a hydrological mechanism. Water comes, enters a course, and emerges as a spring; provision is not static abundance but a routed arrival whose path joins sky and inhabited ground.

#### Subchannel C. Fertility and Yield
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Water-bearing movement culminates in fertile soil, protected growth, and produce carried to maturity.
- Active motifs: fertile planted land (`quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`); sheltered orchard (`quranic:root_000266:B003/m01`); dense plant growth (`quranic:root_000266:B011/m01`); crop and palm yield with abundant water (`quranic:root_000009:B007/m01`); fruit carried on the plant (`quranic:root_000357:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:2 `حَٰمِلَٰتِ` (ح م ل); 51:15 `جَنَّٰتٍ` (ج ن ن); 51:16 `ءَاتَىٰ` (ء ت ي); 51:20,23 `أَرْضِ` (ء ر ض).
- Synthesis: Bearing recurs at the productive end of the supply chain: land receives water, vegetation thickens behind the garden's enclosure, and the plant itself becomes a bearer. Atmospheric carriage is reframed as the first phase of biological yield.

#### Subchannel D. The Allotted Human Share
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Provision is divided, received, held as wealth, and transferred to one whose claim or deprivation makes a share due.
- Active motifs: apportioning shares (`quranic:root_001226:B003/m01`); allotted provision (`quranic:root_000560:B001/m01`); continuing grant (`quranic:root_000240:B006/m01`); taking into possession (`quranic:root_000018:B001/m01`); wealth or property (`quranic:root_001457:B001/m01`); a holder's specific right (`quranic:root_000347:B003/m01`); asking for a share (`quranic:root_000661:B001/m01`); deprivation of expected provision (`quranic:root_000313:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:3 `جَٰرِيَٰتِ` (ج ر ي); 51:4 `مُقَسِّمَٰتِ` (ق س م); 51:16 `ءَاخِذِينَ` (ء خ ذ); 51:19 `أَمْوَٰلِ` (م و ل), `حَقٌّ` (ح ق ق), `سَّآئِلِ` (س ء ل), `مَحْرُومِ` (ح ر م); 51:22 `رِزْقُ` (ر ز ق).
- Synthesis: Cosmic division and ethical giving share one operation: making a measured portion reach its proper recipient. The claimant and the deprived are not appended to the provision scene; they reveal its terminal obligation, where flow becomes possession and possession remains answerable to a right.

### 2. P1 (51:1-23): A Promise Coming Due
- Semantic invariant: A declared commitment moves toward a fixed point at which truth becomes an enforceable account.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:5-6 the truthful promise and the coming reckoning; 51:12-14 the demanded day and tasted trial; 51:22-23 the repeated promise and oath of truth.
- Surprising reach: Promise, debt, appointment, and impact converge on the image of an obligation that does not merely occur but matures into something due.

#### Subchannel A. Commitment, Fulfillment, and Account
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A truthful undertaking is fulfilled when its obligation falls due and enters account.
- Active motifs: binding promise (`quranic:root_001662:B001/m01`); truthful statement (`quranic:root_000852:B001/m01`); fulfillment in action (`quranic:root_000852:B004/m01`); account and recompense (`quranic:root_000504:B002/m01`); debt (`quranic:root_000504:B003/m01`); becoming fixed or due (`quranic:root_001675:B001/m01`); due obligation (`quranic:root_000347:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:5,22 `تُوعَدُ` (و ع د); 51:5 `صَادِقٌ` (ص د ق); 51:6 `وَٰقِعٌ` (و ق ع), `دِّينَ` (د ي ن); 51:19,23 `حَقٌّ` (ح ق ق).
- Synthesis: The promise is construed as more than reliable speech. Its truth is performance, its arrival is a debt becoming due, and the Day of Recompense is the accounting form of that fulfilled commitment.

#### Subchannel B. Appointment and Premature Demand
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A threatened event has an appointed day, while those under warning try to pull that date forward.
- Active motifs: menace within a promise (`quranic:root_001662:B002/m01`); fixed appointment in time (`quranic:root_001662:B003/m01`); day as bounded time (`quranic:root_001700:B001/m01`); decisive event or calamity (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`); haste and premature demand (`quranic:root_000987:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:5,22 `تُوعَدُ` (و ع د); 51:12-13 `يَوْمُ/يَوْمَ` (ي و م); 51:14 `تَسْتَعْجِلُ` (ع ج ل).
- Synthesis: The question about the Day is an attempt to alter the timing of an appointment already contained in the warning. Haste cannot accelerate what is fixed; it only exposes the demander's position beneath the approaching event.

### 3. P1 (51:1-23): Truth Made Legible
- Semantic invariant: Reality discloses itself through ordered form, diagnostic testing, and witnesses within the perceiver.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:7-11 woven heaven, conflicting speech, diversion, conjecture, and heedlessness; 51:13-14 trial by fire; 51:20-23 signs in earth and selves and truth likened to speech.
- Surprising reach: Textile, furnace, and bodily speech are three technologies of verification: fit can be inspected, material can be assayed, and evidence can answer from within.

#### Subchannel A. Woven Order and Distorted Speech
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A tightly interwoven pattern supplies a standard against which divergent, diverted, and conjectural speech appears structurally unfit.
- Active motifs: precise construction (`quranic:root_000290:B001/m01`); interwoven paths (`quranic:root_000290:B002/m01`); tight fastening (`quranic:root_000290:B003/m01`); divergent paths and disagreement (`quranic:root_000433:B004/m01`); spoken utterance (`quranic:root_001272:B001/m01`); diversion from truth (`quranic:root_000041:B001/m01`); unsupported claim (`quranic:root_000403:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:7 `حُبُكِ` (ح ب ك); 51:8 `مُّخْتَلِفٍ` (خ ل ف), `قَوْلٍ` (ق و ل); 51:9 `يُؤْفَكُ/أُفِكَ` (ء ف ك); 51:10 `خَرَّٰصُونَ` (خ ر ص).
- Synthesis: The woven heaven and the conflicting saying are related by fit. One displays paths held in an ordered fabric; the other separates into incompatible routes and then turns hearers away through estimation without knowledge.

#### Subchannel B. Furnace Assay
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Persons and claims are assayed as material is exposed to fire, with tasting supplying direct experiential verification.
- Active motifs: assaying metal or character (`quranic:root_001128:B001/m01`); burning and blackening by fire (`quranic:root_001128:B002/m01`); trial or affliction (`quranic:root_001128:B004/m01`); fire (`quranic:root_001564:B002/m01`); experiential tasting of affliction (`quranic:root_000526:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:13 `يُفْتَنُ` (ف ت ن), `نَّارِ` (ن و ر); 51:14 `فِتْنَتَ` (ف ت ن), `ذُوقُ` (ذ و ق).
- Synthesis: Trial is a furnace operation: exposure reveals what the subject is, while tasting removes the distance of speculation. The demanded judgment becomes knowledge acquired through contact with the test itself.

#### Subchannel C. Embodied Witness
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Signs are inspected in the earth and the self until inner sight and articulate evidence make certainty answerable.
- Active motifs: visible sign (`quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`); fixed certainty (`quranic:root_001696:B001/m01`); eyesight (`quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`); inward insight (`quranic:root_000121:B002/m01`); the self or essence (`quranic:root_001533:B012/m01`); inner reason or intention (`quranic:root_001533:B013/m01`); intelligible speech (`quranic:root_001519:B001/m01`); evidence that speaks by revealing (`quranic:root_001519:B002/m01`); truth fixed to reality (`quranic:root_000347:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:20 `ءَايَٰتٌ` (ء ي ي), `مُوقِنِينَ` (ي ق ن); 51:21 `أَنفُسِ` (ن ف س), `تُبْصِرُ` (ب ص ر); 51:23 `حَقٌّ` (ح ق ق), `تَنطِقُ` (ن ط ق).
- Synthesis: The self is both inspected object and interior faculty of recognition. Sight develops into insight, and the final comparison with speech makes truth function as a witness whose coherence is as immediate as one's own act of speaking.

### 4. P1 (51:1-23): Protective Coverings
- Semantic invariant: Covering creates a protected interior in which life, rest, and repair can occur.
- Surface relation: indirect; 51:15 gardens and springs, 51:17-18 little sleep and predawn forgiveness.
- Surprising reach: The enclosed garden and the covered night share a sheltering function, while forgiveness becomes a moral covering applied at the night's opening edge.

#### Subchannel A. Garden as Refuge
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An enclosure wards harm and holds a watered interior fit for protected life.
- Active motifs: concealment and cover (`quranic:root_000266:B001/m01`); sheltered orchard (`quranic:root_000266:B003/m01`); barrier warding harm (`quranic:root_001677:B001/m01`); placing oneself under protection (`quranic:root_001677:B002/m01`); spring or water source (`quranic:root_001069:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:15 `جَنَّٰتٍ` (ج ن ن), `مُتَّقِينَ` (و ق ي), `عُيُونٍ` (ع ي ن).
- Synthesis: The garden is not only productive land but an enclosure. Protection forms its boundary, springs sustain its interior, and the protected ones inhabit the same sheltering image carried by the garden's root.

#### Subchannel B. Night Cover and Predawn Repair
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Night covers the sleeper, but curtailed sleep opens into a predawn act that covers fault.
- Active motifs: night as a covering (`quranic:root_000266:B002/m01`); night and darkness (`quranic:root_001392:B001/m01`); light sleep (`quranic:root_001579:B001/m01`); predawn (`quranic:root_000682:B004/m01`); covering or protecting (`quranic:root_001096:B001/m01`); forgiveness as covering sin (`quranic:root_001096:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:15 `جَنَّٰتٍ` (ج ن ن); 51:17 `يْلِ` (ل ي ل), `يَهْجَعُ` (ه ج ع); 51:18 `أَسْحَارِ` (س ح ر), `يَسْتَغْفِرُ` (غ ف ر).
- Synthesis: A natural cover and an ethical cover overlap without collapsing. The night permits sleep, restraint leaves part of it wakeful, and at dawn forgiveness supplies the protection that sleep itself cannot provide.

### 5. P2 (51:24-37): Household Reception
- Semantic invariant: A household tests unknown arrivals by threshold, greeting, food, and bodily response.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:24-30 honored guests enter, receive peace and food, trigger fear, and deliver good news.
- Surprising reach: Hospitality becomes an epistemic instrument: an untouched meal and subtle bodily sensing identify the visitors before their message is understood.

#### Subchannel A. Strangers at the Threshold
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Honored outsiders arrive, cross into a household, exchange peace, and remain socially unrecognized.
- Active motifs: arrival and reaching (`quranic:root_000009:B001/m01`); stranger entering another people (`quranic:root_000009:B006/m01`); guest and host relation (`quranic:root_000924:B002/m01`); honor and generosity (`quranic:root_001294:B001/m01`); entry (`quranic:root_000464:B001/m01`); peace and reconciliation (`quranic:root_000737:B004/m01`); unfamiliar person (`quranic:root_001550:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:24 `أَتَىٰ` (ء ت ي), `ضَيْفِ` (ض ي ف), `مُكْرَمِينَ` (ك ر م); 51:25 `دَخَلُ` (د خ ل), `سَلَٰمًا/سَلَٰمٌ` (س ل م), `مُّنكَرُونَ` (ن ك ر).
- Synthesis: Entry changes the visitors from distant arrivals into household participants, but the greeting does not settle their identity. Honor governs Abraham's conduct while unfamiliarity keeps the threshold socially active inside the room.

#### Subchannel B. Preparing and Presenting the Feast
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The host turns privately toward his household, prepares a rich calf, and places it within reach of the guests.
- Active motifs: concealed side movement (`quranic:root_000613:B001/m01`); rolling a morsel in fat (`quranic:root_000613:B003/m01`); household and kin (`quranic:root_000064:B001/m01`); bringing or presenting (`quranic:root_000281:B004/m01`); calf (`quranic:root_000987:B002/m01`); fatness (`quranic:root_000744:B001/m01`); ghee or fatty condiment (`quranic:root_000744:B002/m01`); drawing near (`quranic:root_001212:B001/m01`); eating (`quranic:root_000043:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:26 `رَاغَ` (ر و غ), `أَهْلِ` (ء ه ل), `جَآءَ` (ج ي ء), `عِجْلٍ` (ع ج ل), `سَمِينٍ` (س م ن); 51:27 `قَرَّبَ` (ق ر ب), `تَأْكُلُ` (ء ك ل).
- Synthesis: The meal is a complete hospitality mechanism: discreet withdrawal protects generosity from display, the household supplies the prepared animal, and presentation converts possession into an offered share placed near enough to eat.

#### Subchannel C. The Untouched Meal as Signal
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Failure to eat is detected through slight signs, becomes inward fear, and is answered by speech of reassurance and good news.
- Active motifs: eating (`quranic:root_000043:B001/m01`); tasting food little by little (`quranic:root_001627:B004/m01`); hidden sound (`quranic:root_001627:B001/m01`); listening for a hidden sound (`quranic:root_001627:B001/m02`); fear or intuition in the heart (`quranic:root_001627:B002/m01`); fear (`quranic:root_000447:B001/m01`); good news opening the face with joy (`quranic:root_000120:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:27 `تَأْكُلُ` (ء ك ل); 51:28 `أَوْجَسَ` (و ج س), `خِيفَةً/تَخَفْ` (خ و ف), `بَشَّرُ` (ب ش ر).
- Synthesis: The guests' abstention makes the meal diagnostic. Abraham's sensing is rendered as attention to barely perceptible evidence, and reassurance reverses that inward alarm into the outward opening of good news.

### 6. P2 (51:24-37): Birth at the Threshold of Impossibility
- Semantic invariant: A body marked as closed to reproduction is approached as a site of imminent reception.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:28-30 a knowledgeable son is announced to an aged, barren woman.
- Surprising reach: The wife's approach, cry, face, and striking hand activate a delivery scene in which the woman who cannot conceive is lexically positioned to receive a child.

#### Subchannel A. Aged and Barren, Yet Addressed by Birth
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Good news names a child against the embodied evidence of age and barrenness.
- Active motifs: good news (`quranic:root_000120:B005/m01`); boy or young son (`quranic:root_001103:B001/m01`); knowledge (`quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`); woman (`quranic:root_001409:B001/m01`); old woman or old age (`quranic:root_000985:B003/m01`); a child born late in the parents' age (`quranic:root_000985:B007/m01`); barren womb (`quranic:root_001037:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:28 `بَشَّرُ` (ب ش ر), `غُلَٰمٍ` (غ ل م), `عَلِيمٍ` (ع ل م); 51:29 `ٱمْرَأَتُ` (م ر ء), `عَجُوزٌ` (ع ج ز), `عَقِيمٌ` (ع ق م); 51:30 `عَلِيمُ` (ع ل م).
- Synthesis: The announcement confronts two closures, age and barrenness, with a specifically characterized son. Knowledge belongs both to the promised child and to the authority of the promise, displacing bodily probability without denying the body's condition.

#### Subchannel B. The Body as a Delivery Scene
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Approach, cry, hand-to-face movement, and reception assemble the mechanics surrounding childbirth.
- Active motifs: approaching birth (`quranic:root_001212:B012/m01`); midwife receiving a child (`quranic:root_001198:B007/m01`); cry or shriek (`quranic:root_000856:B004/m01`); forceful collision or slap (`quranic:root_000874:B001/m01`); hands-first delivery (`quranic:root_001630:B010/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:27 `قَرَّبَ` (ق ر ب); 51:29 `أَقْبَلَتِ` (ق ب ل), `صَرَّةٍ` (ص ر ر), `صَكَّتْ` (ص ك ك), `وَجْهَ` (و ج ه).
- Synthesis: The surface gesture of astonishment is simultaneously populated by childbirth operations. The wife's coming forward and cry move toward a scene of reception, while hand and face imagery press the impossible announcement into concrete bodily mechanics.

### 7. P2 (51:24-37): Authorized Targeting
- Semantic invariant: Judgment moves from sovereign decision to documented authorization, marked material, and a bounded class of offenders.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:30-34 the wise Lord's envoys identify their commission and its marked clay stones for transgressors.
- Surprising reach: Writing, sealing, marking, and projectile material turn the decree into a physical consignment whose destination is already inscribed.

#### Subchannel A. The Sealed Warrant
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A sovereign decision becomes delegated authority, a written instrument, and a sealed dispatch carried by envoys.
- Active motifs: judicial decision (`quranic:root_000348:B002/m01`); delegated adjudication or authorization (`quranic:root_000348:B005/m01`); sovereign or master (`quranic:root_000532:B001/m01`); grave affair (`quranic:root_000421:B003/m01`); dispatch (`quranic:root_000563:B001/m01`); messenger and message (`quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`); written deed or document (`quranic:root_000874:B006/m01`); clay seal (`quranic:root_000963:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:29 `صَكَّتْ` (ص ك ك); 51:30 `حَكِيمُ` (ح ك م), `رَبُّ` (ر ب ب); 51:31 `خَطْبُ` (خ ط ب), `مُرْسَلُونَ` (ر س ل); 51:32-33 `أُرْسِلْ/نُرْسِلَ` (ر س ل); 51:33 `طِينٍ` (ط ي ن).
- Synthesis: The envoys' commission reads as an authorized instrument. Wisdom decides, lordship authorizes, dispatch transmits, and the latent deed-and-seal imagery gives the spoken mission the material integrity of a warrant.

#### Subchannel B. Marked Clay-Stone Consignment
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Clay is hardened into stones, marked, held before authority, and released toward designated recipients.
- Active motifs: clay material (`quranic:root_000963:B001/m01`); hard stone (`quranic:root_000296:B003/m01`); marking or inscription (`quranic:root_000764:B004/m01`); held or present before authority (`quranic:root_001052:B004/m01`); dispatch (`quranic:root_000563:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:31-33 `مُرْسَلُونَ/أُرْسِلْ/نُرْسِلَ` (ر س ل); 51:33 `حِجَارَةً` (ح ج ر), `طِينٍ` (ط ي ن); 51:34 `مُّسَوَّمَةً` (س و م), `عِندَ` (ع ن د).
- Synthesis: Material, manufacture, custody, and destination form one delivery chain. The stones are not undifferentiated debris; their marking makes each projectile a targeted unit held under authority before dispatch.

#### Subchannel C. Offense Beyond the Boundary
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A prohibited boundary is crossed, defining the offending population to whom the judgment is addressed.
- Active motifs: offense or crime (`quranic:root_000239:B004/m01`); prohibition and restraint (`quranic:root_000296:B001/m01`); exceeding bounds (`quranic:root_000699:B001/m01`); judicial decision (`quranic:root_000348:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:30 `حَكِيمُ` (ح ك م); 51:32 `مُّجْرِمِينَ` (ج ر م); 51:33 `حِجَارَةً` (ح ج ر); 51:34 `مُسْرِفِينَ` (س ر ف).
- Synthesis: The target class is produced by a boundary relation: restraint establishes a limit, excess crosses it, and crime names the resulting status. Judgment and projectile destination therefore share the same act of specification.

### 8. P2 (51:24-37): Selective Extraction and Retained Trace
- Semantic invariant: Judgment sorts a site by removing protected occupants while preserving an evidentiary remainder.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:35-37 believers are brought out, one submitting household is found, and a sign is left for those who fear punishment.
- Surprising reach: Extraction and residue are complementary archival operations: living witnesses are removed from danger while the damaged place is retained as testimony.

#### Subchannel A. Removing the Protected Household
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A search identifies the secure household and extracts its believing occupants from concealment and danger.
- Active motifs: going out (`quranic:root_000400:B001/m01`); extracting from concealment (`quranic:root_000400:B002/m01`); security (`quranic:root_000054:B001/m01`); belief and trust (`quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`); finding (`quranic:root_001626:B001/m01`); dwelling or refuge (`quranic:root_000166:B001/m01`); safety and integrity (`quranic:root_000737:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:35 `أَخْرَجْ` (خ ر ج), `مُؤْمِنِينَ` (ء م ن); 51:36 `وَجَدْ` (و ج د), `بَيْتٍ` (ب ي ت), `مُسْلِمِينَ` (س ل م).
- Synthesis: Removal is selective rather than indiscriminate. Finding precedes extraction, and the household functions simultaneously as dwelling, refuge, and the social container in which trust and safety coincide.

#### Subchannel B. Leaving the Site as Witness
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: After extraction, a trace remains to make pain and punishment visible to later fear.
- Active motifs: retained trace or memory (`quranic:root_000180:B002/m01`); visible sign (`quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`); fear (`quranic:root_000447:B001/m01`); punishment (`quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`); causing pain (`quranic:root_000046:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:37 `تَرَكْ` (ت ر ك), `ءَايَةً` (ء ي ي), `يَخَافُ` (خ و ف), `عَذَابَ` (ع ذ ب), `أَلِيمَ` (ء ل م).
- Synthesis: What is left is not accidental residue. The trace is curated by survival of place: pain becomes visible, visibility can generate fear, and the judged site continues to act after its inhabitants have been removed.

### 9. P3 (51:38-60): Rejected Authority and Reversed Support
- Semantic invariant: A ruler's attempt to resist proof through retinue and labels ends with his own force being seized and discarded.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:38-40 Moses brings manifest authority, Pharaoh turns with his support, labels him, and is cast into the sea with his forces.
- Surprising reach: Political support is exposed as architectural but unstable: the ruler leans on a strong side until seizure reverses agent and object.

#### Subchannel A. Envoy, Proof, and the Ruler's Strong Side
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An envoy arrives with compelling authority while the ruler turns away by leaning on a retinue imagined as structural support.
- Active motifs: messenger and message (`quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`); compelling proof (`quranic:root_000732:B002/m01`); authority and power (`quranic:root_000732:B003/m01`); manifest clarity (`quranic:root_000170:B004/m01`); exposition (`quranic:root_000170:B005/m01`); turning away (`quranic:root_001684:B007/m01`); strong side, support, or retinue (`quranic:root_000596:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:38 `أَرْسَلْ` (ر س ل), `سُلْطَٰنٍ` (س ل ط), `مُّبِينٍ` (ب ي ن); 51:39 `تَوَلَّىٰ` (و ل ي), `رُكْنِ` (ر ك ن).
- Synthesis: Two forms of authority confront each other. Moses' authority is evidentiary and self-manifesting; Pharaoh's is positional, a side on which he leans. Turning away is thus an enacted preference for support over proof.

#### Subchannel B. Defensive Labels
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Speech replaces engagement with proof by classifying the envoy as either deceptive or mentally veiled.
- Active motifs: spoken utterance (`quranic:root_001272:B001/m01`); sorcery as deceptive diversion (`quranic:root_000682:B002/m01`); madness as a veiled mind (`quranic:root_000266:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:39 `قَالَ` (ق و ل), `سَٰحِرٌ` (س ح ر), `مَجْنُونٌ` (ج ن ن).
- Synthesis: The paired labels offer incompatible diagnoses but perform the same defense. One blames calculated manipulation and the other incapacity, allowing speech to displace the manifest authority it cannot answer.

#### Subchannel C. Capture and Casting Reversal
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The ruler who relied on his force becomes the object of seizure, and his army is discarded into the sea under blame.
- Active motifs: forcible seizure (`quranic:root_000018:B001/m02`); capture (`quranic:root_000018:B003/m01`); army (`quranic:root_000264:B001/m01`); casting away (`quranic:root_001466:B001/m01`); sea (`quranic:root_001697:B003/m01`); blame and culpability (`quranic:root_001386:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:40 `أَخَذْ` (ء خ ذ), `جُنُودَ` (ج ن د), `نَبَذْ` (ن ب ذ), `يَمِّ` (ي م م), `مُلِيمٌ` (ل و م).
- Synthesis: Agency flips completely. The commander and his instruments become a single captured object, while casting turns the force that was meant to uphold him into discarded material.

### 10. P3 (51:38-60): Elemental Unmaking and Lost Capacity
- Semantic invariant: Released force overtakes a population by progressively removing form, time, stance, and aid.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:41-45 a sterile wind reduces things to decay, a respite ends at the Lord's command, and a blast leaves no capacity to stand or prevail.
- Surprising reach: Wind and thunder do not merely destroy objects; they dismantle the predicates of agency, from remaining intact to standing and receiving help.

#### Subchannel A. Sterile Wind to Residue
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A released but nonproductive wind reaches each thing, abandons it, and converts it into decayed remains.
- Active motifs: release of a natural force (`quranic:root_000563:B001/m02`); wind (`quranic:root_000609:B003/m01`); sterility (`quranic:root_001037:B002/m01`); leaving or abandoning (`quranic:root_001638:B003/m01`); arrival and reaching (`quranic:root_000009:B001/m01`); transformation into a state (`quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`); decayed remains (`quranic:root_000601:B004/m01`); entity or thing (`quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:41 `أَرْسَلْ` (ر س ل), `رِّيحَ` (ر و ح), `عَقِيمَ` (ع ق م); 51:42 `أَتَتْ` (ء ت ي), `تَذَرُ` (و ذ ر), `جَعَلَتْ` (ج ع ل), `رَّمِيمِ` (ر م م), `شَىْءٍ` (ش ي ء).
- Synthesis: Sterility names the wind's output as well as its nature: it produces nothing living and leaves nothing productive. Arrival, abandonment, and transformation form a single operation that converts differentiated things into one residue.

#### Subchannel B. The Measured Respite
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Enjoyment occupies a bounded interval before defiant excess meets the governing command.
- Active motifs: respite for enjoyment (`quranic:root_001395:B007/m01`); bounded interval (`quranic:root_000382:B001/m01`); defiant overreach (`quranic:root_000981:B001/m01`); command (`quranic:root_000051:B002/m01`); sovereign or master (`quranic:root_000532:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:43 `تَمَتَّعُ` (م ت ع), `حِينٍ` (ح ي ن); 51:44 `عَتَ` (ع ت و), `أَمْرِ` (ء م ر), `رَبِّ` (ر ب ب).
- Synthesis: The interval is permissive but not ownerless. Enjoyment fills measured time, overreach mistakes delay for independence, and the command reveals that the respite always remained bounded by another authority.

#### Subchannel C. Blast, Collapse, and Failed Aid
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A thunderous seizure overtakes those still watching, stuns them, removes standing capacity, and forecloses rescue.
- Active motifs: forcible seizure (`quranic:root_000018:B001/m02`); thunderclap or lightning strike (`quranic:root_000864:B002/m01`); stunned or dead state (`quranic:root_000864:B003/m01`); watching (`quranic:root_001520:B001/m01`); ability (`quranic:root_000956:B003/m01`); standing (`quranic:root_001273:B002/m01`); stopped or exhausted incapacity (`quranic:root_001273:B016/m01`); aid (`quranic:root_001510:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:44 `أَخَذَتْ` (ء خ ذ), `صَّٰعِقَةُ` (ص ع ق), `يَنظُرُ` (ن ظ ر); 51:45 `ٱسْتَطَٰعُ` (ط و ع), `قِيَامٍ` (ق و م), `مُنتَصِرِينَ` (ن ص ر).
- Synthesis: The blast attacks agency in sequence. Observation remains for an instant, then seizure converts witnesses into the stunned; ability contracts into inability to stand, and social reinforcement disappears with the impossibility of aid.

### 11. P3 (51:38-60): Cosmic Habitation
- Semantic invariant: Creation is made habitable through complementary construction above and furnishing below.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:47 the strengthened, expanding built sky and 51:48 the spread earth and excellent preparation.
- Surprising reach: The sky carries the anatomy of ribs and supports, while the earth carries the domestic vocabulary of bed and cradle.

#### Subchannel A. Built and Widened Overhead Structure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An overhead canopy is constructed with force, load-bearing supports, and continuing spatial capacity.
- Active motifs: construction (`quranic:root_000156:B001/m01`); ribs, pillars, or load supports (`quranic:root_000156:B009/m01`); overhead canopy (`quranic:root_000745:B004/m01`); force and strength (`quranic:root_000071:B001/m01`); spatial breadth (`quranic:root_001647:B001/m01`); capacity (`quranic:root_001647:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:47 `بَنَيْ` (ب ن ي), `سَّمَآءَ` (س م و), `أَيْي۟دٍ` (ء ي د), `مُوسِعُونَ` (و س ع).
- Synthesis: Building and widening are one architectural process rather than competing images. Strength supplies the structure, rib-like supports distribute its load, and breadth describes the capacity of the completed canopy.

#### Subchannel B. Furnished and Leveled Ground
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Ground is spread, smoothed, and prepared as bedding and cradle for habitation.
- Active motifs: ground or earth (`quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`); spreading and preparing a surface (`quranic:root_001143:B001/m01`); bedding or furnishing (`quranic:root_001143:B002/m01`); praise and approval (`quranic:root_001525:B003/m01`); cradle (`quranic:root_001451:B001/m01`); prepared bed or smooth ground (`quranic:root_001451:B002/m01`); leveling and preparation (`quranic:root_001451:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:48 `أَرْضَ` (ء ر ض), `فَرَشْ` (ف ر ش), `نِعْمَ` (ن ع م), `مَٰهِدُونَ` (م ه د).
- Synthesis: The earth is not only extended but furnished. Spreading provides area, leveling makes it usable, and the bed-cradle imagery defines excellence by the ground's readiness to receive and sustain inhabitants.

### 12. P3 (51:38-60): Competing Transmissions
- Semantic invariant: Communities inherit a repeatable discourse of refusal, while reminder interrupts that transmission with beneficial recall.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:52-53 earlier peoples repeat the labels “sorcerer” and “madman” as if by mutual counsel; 51:54-55 turning away is released from blame and reminder continues for believers.
- Surprising reach: The text treats rejection as a social inheritance and remembrance as a counter-transmission rather than as isolated individual opinions.

#### Subchannel A. Inherited Formula of Dismissal
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Each arriving messenger meets a circulating formula passed along across prior communities through reciprocal counsel.
- Active motifs: arrival (`quranic:root_000009:B001/m01`); prior sequence (`quranic:root_001198:B002/m01`); messenger (`quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`); circulating saying (`quranic:root_001272:B007/m01`); sorcery (`quranic:root_000682:B002/m01`); madness (`quranic:root_000266:B006/m01`); reciprocal counsel or bequeathing (`quranic:root_001656:B003/m01`); social group (`quranic:root_001273:B001/m01`); transgression (`quranic:root_000937:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:52 `أَتَى` (ء ت ي), `قَبْلِ` (ق ب ل), `رَّسُولٍ` (ر س ل), `قَالُ` (ق و ل), `سَاحِرٌ` (س ح ر), `مَجْنُونٌ` (ج ن ن); 51:53 `تَوَاصَ` (و ص ي), `قَوْمٌ` (ق و م), `طَاغُونَ` (ط غ ي).
- Synthesis: Recurrence is presented as though communities had transmitted a script to one another. The same labels circulate because the shared act is not diagnosis but refusal; reciprocal counsel gives temporal continuity to transgression.

#### Subchannel B. Corrective Reminder
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: After disengagement from entrenched refusal, repeated mention reactivates useful knowledge among those able to trust it.
- Active motifs: turning away (`quranic:root_001684:B007/m01`); blame (`quranic:root_001386:B001/m01`); recall (`quranic:root_000516:B003/m01`); spoken mention (`quranic:root_000516:B004/m01`); reminder (`quranic:root_000516:B009/m01`); benefit (`quranic:root_001536:B001/m01`); belief and trust (`quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:54 `تَوَلَّ` (و ل ي), `مَلُومٍ` (ل و م); 51:55 `ذَكِّرْ/ذِّكْرَىٰ` (ذ ك ر), `تَنفَعُ` (ن ف ع), `مُؤْمِنِينَ` (ء م ن).
- Synthesis: Turning away ends an unproductive exchange but not communication itself. Reminder is the alternative circulation: mention repeatedly returns knowledge to attention, and benefit occurs where trust allows that transmission to be received.

### 13. P3 (51:38-60): Asymmetric Service and Provision
- Semantic invariant: Created beings are oriented toward service, whereas the Creator's provision and strength exclude reciprocal need.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:56 jinn and humans are created to worship, and 51:57-58 no provision or feeding is sought from them because God is the strong Provider.
- Surprising reach: The servant relation is clarified economically: worship cannot be payment, food supply, or reinforcement for the one who already supplies and sustains.

#### Subchannel A. Created Beings Assigned to Service
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Hidden and visible beings are created within a servant relation whose proper operation is worshipful obedience.
- Active motifs: creation (`quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`); hidden jinn (`quranic:root_000266:B005/m01`); visible human being (`quranic:root_000059:B001/m01`); servitude relation (`quranic:root_000973:B001/m01`); worshipful obedience (`quranic:root_000973:B003/m01`); subjecting another to service (`quranic:root_000973:B004/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:56 `خَلَقْ` (خ ل ق), `جِنَّ` (ج ن ن), `إِنسَ` (ء ن س), `يَعْبُدُ` (ع ب د).
- Synthesis: Jinn and humans differ in visibility but occupy the same created role. The lexical range of service makes worship concrete as directed obedience while the creation relation prevents the servant from becoming the source of the master's sufficiency.

#### Subchannel B. The Provider Who Does Not Need Feeding
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Desire for incoming provision and food is denied, while outgoing allotment, feeding power, and durable strength are affirmed.
- Active motifs: desire or will (`quranic:root_000610:B001/m01`); allotted provision (`quranic:root_000560:B001/m01`); food (`quranic:root_000560:B002/m01`); feeding another (`quranic:root_000934:B002/m01`); deity and object of worship (`quranic:root_000047:B001/m01`); strength (`quranic:root_001274:B001/m01`); toughness and solidity (`quranic:root_001396:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:57 `أُرِيدُ` (ر و د), `رِّزْقٍ` (ر ز ق), `يُطْعِمُ` (ط ع م); 51:58 `ٱللَّهَ` (ء ل ه), `رَّزَّاقُ` (ر ز ق), `قُوَّةِ` (ق و ي), `مَتِينُ` (م ت ن).
- Synthesis: Provision has a fixed direction. The deity does not occupy the recipient's side of the food relation; strength and solidity identify the source from which allotment and feeding proceed without replenishment from servants.

### 14. P3 (51:38-60): Measured Turns at the Well
- Semantic invariant: A due portion arrives as a full, comparable share on an appointed turn that cannot be hurried.
- Surface relation: indirect; 51:59 the wrongdoers have a `ذَنُوبًا` like their companions' and are told not to hasten it; 51:60 fixes the deniers' promised day.
- Surprising reach: The threatened “share” becomes a full water bucket drawn in sequence, turning judgment into an allotted turn at a communal well.

#### Subchannel A. The Comparable Full Bucket
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Members of a culpable group receive an equal, full bucket as their allotted portion.
- Active motifs: grievance and liability (`quranic:root_000967:B003/m01`); withholding or violating rights (`quranic:root_000967:B008/m01`); allotted portion (`quranic:root_000521:B006/m01`); full water bucket (`quranic:root_000521:B007/m01`); equality or likeness (`quranic:root_001397:B001/m03`); companions as a group (`quranic:root_000844:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:59 `ظَلَمُ` (ظ ل م), `ذَنُوبًا/ذَنُوبِ` (ذ ن ب), `مِّثْلَ` (م ث ل), `أَصْحَٰبِ` (ص ح ب).
- Synthesis: The surface “share” retains the concrete mechanics of drawing water: a bucket is filled, assigned, and compared with the portions of earlier companions. Equality here does not lessen judgment; it specifies its measured completeness.

#### Subchannel B. The Turn That Cannot Be Hurried
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The recipient tries to accelerate a threatening allotment whose date is already fixed.
- Active motifs: haste (`quranic:root_000987:B001/m01`); menace within a promise (`quranic:root_001662:B002/m01`); fixed appointment (`quranic:root_001662:B003/m01`); day as bounded time (`quranic:root_001700:B001/m01`); decisive event (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`); concealing truth (`quranic:root_001307:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:59 `يَسْتَعْجِلُ` (ع ج ل); 51:60 `يُوعَدُ` (و ع د), `يَوْمِ` (ي و م), `كَفَرُ` (ك ف ر).
- Synthesis: Haste is futile because the portion belongs to an ordered sequence and the promise carries its own appointment. The final Day is therefore the temporal equivalent of the full bucket: a measured allotment arriving exactly on its turn.

### 15. P3 (51:38-60): Created Correspondence
- Semantic invariant: Measured creation produces forms that become intelligible through relation to a counterpart.
- Surface relation: direct; 51:49 all things are created in pairs so that the hearer may remember.
- Surprising reach: Pairing operates both as a taxonomy of comparable kinds and as a reproductive relation, while the root of remembrance itself supplies the latent male term.

#### Subchannel A. Designed Counterparts and Kinds
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Things are measured into finished forms and arranged as counterparts, classes, and peers.
- Active motifs: the whole with all its parts (`quranic:root_001315:B003/m01`); entity or thing (`quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`); pre-measuring and cutting to design (`quranic:root_000434:B001/m01`); creation (`quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`); finished form (`quranic:root_000434:B003/m01`); counterpart pair (`quranic:root_000652:B001/m01`); class or type (`quranic:root_000652:B004/m01`); peers by form or action (`quranic:root_000652:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:49 `كُلِّ` (ك ل ل), `شَىْءٍ` (ش ي ء), `خَلَقْ` (خ ل ق), `زَوْجَيْنِ` (ز و ج).
- Synthesis: Pairing first functions as an ordering principle. Measured formation gives each thing a definite shape, while counterpart, class, and peer relations make that shape legible within a structured plurality.

#### Subchannel B. Reproductive Pair and Recall
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A spousal counterpart and a male term activate reproduction inside the command to recover recognition.
- Active motifs: creation (`quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`); spousal pair (`quranic:root_000652:B002/m01`); male (`quranic:root_000516:B001/m01`); recall (`quranic:root_000516:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:49 `خَلَقْ` (خ ل ق), `زَوْجَيْنِ` (ز و ج), `تَذَكَّرُ` (ذ ك ر).
- Synthesis: The reproductive sense is a distinct operation within created correspondence. The latent male sense in the same root as remembering lets sexual complementarity become a mnemonic pressure: recognizing the pair is itself an act of recollection.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. P3 (51:38-60): Flight into Exclusive Refuge
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An alarm triggers flight toward one manifest refuge while every alternative deity is refused constructed status.
- Active motifs: fleeing (`quranic:root_001142:B001/m01`); deity and object of worship (`quranic:root_000047:B001/m01`); assigning or creating a status (`quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`); another or alternative (`quranic:root_000019:B001/m01`); alarm and warning (`quranic:root_001488:B001/m01`); manifest clarity (`quranic:root_000170:B004/m01`); explanatory clarity (`quranic:root_000170:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:50 `فِرُّ` (ف ر ر), `ٱللَّهِ` (ء ل ه), `نَذِيرٌ` (ن ذ ر), `مُّبِينٌ` (ب ي ن); 51:51 `تَجْعَلُ` (ج ع ل), `ءَاخَرَ` (ء خ ر), `ٱللَّهِ/إِلَٰهًا` (ء ل ه), `نَذِيرٌ` (ن ذ ر), `مُّبِينٌ` (ب ي ن).
- Synthesis: Flight is not undirected escape but a movement whose destination is determined by exclusive deity. The warning supplies urgency, clarity supplies orientation, and the prohibition on making another god closes every rival refuge.

### S2. Whole Surah: The Maysir Draw and Camel Shares
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Players take marked arrows from a leather holder, draw a named fifth arrow, slaughter a camel, and distribute edible portions according to the lot.
- Active motifs: leather holder or collected set of gambling arrows (`quranic:root_000532:B010/m01`); the fifth maysir arrow (`quranic:root_001533:B016/m01`); maysir players, arrows, camel slaughter, and division by lots (`quranic:root_001694:B007/m01`); edible allotted share (`quranic:root_000043:B003/m01`); fortune or luck (`quranic:root_000560:B006/m01`); apportioning shares (`quranic:root_001226:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:3 `يُسْرًا` (ي س ر); 51:4 `مُقَسِّمَٰتِ` (ق س م); 51:16,23,30,34,44 `رَبُّ/رَبِّ` (ر ب ب); 51:21 `أَنفُسِ` (ن ف س); 51:22,57-58 `رِزْقُ/رِّزْقٍ/رَّزَّاقُ` (ر ز ق); 51:27 `تَأْكُلُ` (ء ك ل).
- Synthesis: The cluster forms a complete social practice in which a container, specialized arrow, players, animal, draw, and resulting food share all have distinct roles. It usefully pressures the surah's primary language of division, provision, and appointed portions: human allotment can depend on luck, whereas the promised reckoning and divine provision are repeatedly presented as truthful, measured, and fixed rather than chancy.

### S3. Whole Surah: Hide Made into a Waterskin
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hairy hide is stripped, treated with plant tannin in measured applications, stitched at its edges, bound, and tested as a water container for leaks.
- Active motifs: stripping the skin from a surface (`quranic:root_000120:B004/m01`); hide kept with hair or wool attached (`quranic:root_000844:B006/m01`); tree bark or leaves used for tanning (`quranic:root_000737:B008/m01`); a small measured application of tanning agent (`quranic:root_001533:B007/m01`); stitching one hide edge to another (`quranic:root_000121:B006/m01`); leather waterskin (`quranic:root_001212:B009/m01`); thin spot or leaking hole in a skin (`quranic:root_001069:B007/m01`); binding and strengthening a waterskin (`quranic:root_001396:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:15 `عُيُونٍ` (ع ي ن); 51:21 `أَنفُسِ` (ن ف س), `تُبْصِرُ` (ب ص ر); 51:25,36 `سَلَٰمًا/سَلَٰمٌ/مُسْلِمِينَ` (س ل م); 51:27 `قَرَّبَ` (ق ر ب); 51:28 `بَشَّرُ` (ب ش ر); 51:58 `مَتِينُ` (م ت ن); 51:59 `أَصْحَٰبِ` (ص ح ب).
- Synthesis: These motifs describe a continuous craft sequence from raw hide to serviceable vessel, including material, tanning agent, dosage, seam, reinforcement, and failure point. The scene materializes the surah's water, hospitality, and provision readings as created dependence on skilled preservation and carriage; it sharpens the closing contrast with the Provider, whose strength and giving require neither vessel nor replenishment.

### S4. Whole Surah: Herds Driven to Water
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Camels move toward water at night, arrive herd after herd, wait through alternating drawing turns, drink at the mouth of the well, re-enter for a second drink, and return at evening with milk as the pastoral yield.
- Active motifs: herds sent successively to pasture or water (`quranic:root_000563:B005/m01`); night drive toward the watering place (`quranic:root_001212:B008/m01`); water drawing by alternating turns (`quranic:root_000433:B011/m01`); watering at camels' mouths (`quranic:root_001198:B014/m01`); returning camels to drink again (`quranic:root_000464:B007/m01`); evening movement back to the resting enclosure (`quranic:root_000609:B005/m01`); camels and grazing livestock (`quranic:root_001525:B005/m01`); recurring milk yield (`quranic:root_000563:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:8 `مُّخْتَلِفٍ` (خ ل ف); 51:16,29,46,52 `قَبْلَ/أَقْبَلَتِ/قَبْلُ/قَبْلِ` (ق ب ل); 51:25 `دَخَلُ` (د خ ل); 51:27 `قَرَّبَ` (ق ر ب); 51:31-33,38,41,52 `مُرْسَلُونَ/أُرْسِلْ/نُرْسِلَ/أَرْسَلْ/رَّسُولٍ` (ر س ل); 51:41 `رِّيحَ` (ر و ح); 51:48 `نِعْمَ` (ن ع م).
- Synthesis: The motifs complete a timed husbandry cycle with animals, route, destination, cooperative labor, repeated access, return, and productive outcome. This materializes the surah's opening motions and recurring dispatch language as regulated provision: what is “sent” comes in ordered succession, reaches a shared source by turns, and returns as sustenance rather than as undirected movement.

### S5. Whole Surah: Dry Ablution and Directed Prayer
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A worshipper uses clean earth to wipe face and hands, then turns the face toward the prayer direction for embodied service.
- Active motifs: prayer direction or qibla (`quranic:root_001198:B005/m01`); face, orientation, and destination (`quranic:root_001630:B002/m01`); dry ablution by wiping face and hands with earth (`quranic:root_001697:B002/m01`); worshipful obedience (`quranic:root_000973:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:16,29,46,52 `قَبْلَ/أَقْبَلَتِ/قَبْلُ/قَبْلِ` (ق ب ل); 51:29 `وَجْهَ` (و ج ه); 51:40 `يَمِّ` (ي م م); 51:56 `يَعْبُدُ` (ع ب د).
- Synthesis: The four motifs form a compact rite of preparation, bodily orientation, and worship. It materializes “flee to God” and the purpose of worship as directed practice rather than abstract inclination: earth prepares the body, the face establishes destination, and service gives the turn its operative form.

### S6. Whole Surah: Ostrich Pair, Egg, and Chick
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A male ostrich and its counterpart produce an egg left in the open, from which a chick forms and emerges.
- Active motifs: ostrich as male or female bird (`quranic:root_001525:B006/m01`); male ostrich (`quranic:root_000967:B006/m01`); ostrich egg left in the wilderness (`quranic:root_000180:B008/m01`); ostrich chick (`quranic:root_000248:B010/m01`); chick emerging from its egg (`quranic:root_001274:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:37 `تَرَكْ` (ت ر ك); 51:42,51 `جَعَلَتْ/تَجْعَلُ` (ج ع ل); 51:48 `نِعْمَ` (ن ع م); 51:58 `قُوَّةِ` (ق و ي); 51:59 `ظَلَمُ` (ظ ل م).
- Synthesis: Adult, sexed counterpart, egg, offspring, and hatching make this a complete desert reproduction scene rather than a bird inventory. It supports and concretizes the surah's primary sequence of impossible birth, sterile force, and created pairs: even an exposed egg on open ground carries a formed life that breaks from its shell, setting reproductive emergence against barrenness and reduction to residue.

### S7. P1-P2 Bridge: The Earth Becomes an Archive
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: P1-P2 role-progression bridge; the sign-and-perception scene remains canonically placed in Parent 3, Subchannel C, and the retained-trace scene in Parent 8, Subchannel B. This entry places only their transition from discoverable evidence to a deliberately preserved historical witness.
- Active motifs: visible sign (`quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`); fixed certainty (`quranic:root_001696:B001/m01`); inward insight (`quranic:root_000121:B002/m01`); retained trace or memory (`quranic:root_000180:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: P1 51:20 `ءَايَٰتٌ` (ء ي ي), `مُوقِنِينَ` (ي ق ن); P1 51:21 `تُبْصِرُ` (ب ص ر); P2 51:37 `تَرَكْ` (ت ر ك), `ءَايَةً` (ء ي ي).
- Synthesis: The bridge is a role progression in evidence. P1 asks the perceiver to find signs already present in earth and self; P2 shows judgment producing a trace and then preserving it as a sign for later perceivers. The earth therefore moves from field of observation to archive, while the local perception and ruin scenes retain their separate canonical placements.

### S8. P2-P3 Bridge: Sterility Reversed
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: P2-P3 same-root contrast; the impossible-birth scene remains canonically placed in Parent 6, Subchannel A, and the sterile-wind scene in Parent 10, Subchannel A. This entry places only the reversal in what sterility is allowed to produce.
- Active motifs: barren womb (`quranic:root_001037:B001/m01`); boy or young son (`quranic:root_001103:B001/m01`); sterile wind (`quranic:root_001037:B002/m01`); decayed remains (`quranic:root_000601:B004/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: P2 51:28 `غُلَٰمٍ` (غ ل م); P2 51:29 `عَقِيمٌ` (ع ق م); P3 51:41 `عَقِيمَ` (ع ق م); P3 51:42 `رَّمِيمِ` (ر م م).
- Synthesis: The bridge is an exact lexical reversal around `ع ق م`. In P2, barrenness describes a body whose expected outcome is overridden by the promised son; in P3, sterility describes a wind whose defining outcome is that nothing living follows it. The same closure to generation is defeated in one scene and weaponized in the other.

### S9. P2-P3 Bridge: Non-Consumption Discloses Agency
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: P2-P3 repeated-scene and role-progression bridge; the untouched-meal scene remains canonically placed in Parent 5, Subchannel C, and divine provision in Parent 13, Subchannel B. This entry places only the disclosure produced when an expected recipient does not consume food.
- Active motifs: eating (`quranic:root_000043:B001/m01`); tasting food (`quranic:root_001627:B004/m01`); desire or will (`quranic:root_000610:B001/m01`); food (`quranic:root_000560:B002/m01`); feeding another (`quranic:root_000934:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: P2 51:27 `تَأْكُلُ` (ء ك ل); P2 51:28 `أَوْجَسَ` (و ج س); P3 51:57 `أُرِيدُ` (ر و د), `رِّزْقٍ` (ر ز ق), `يُطْعِمُ` (ط ع م).
- Synthesis: The bridge is a repeated interaction signature with a change of scale. In P2, food is placed before apparent guests and their non-consumption reveals that ordinary guest roles do not apply; in P3, the denial of desired provision and feeding makes that asymmetry explicit for the Creator. Absence of eating first discloses messenger nature and then defines divine non-need.

### S10. P2-P3 Bridge: Opposite Removals
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: P2-P3 contrast-and-reversal bridge; protected extraction remains canonically placed in Parent 8, Subchannel A, and capture and casting in Parent 9, Subchannel C. This entry places only the shared removal operation and its opposed destinations.
- Active motifs: extracting from concealment (`quranic:root_000400:B002/m01`); safety and integrity (`quranic:root_000737:B001/m01`); forcible seizure (`quranic:root_000018:B001/m02`); capture (`quranic:root_000018:B003/m01`); casting away (`quranic:root_001466:B001/m01`); sea (`quranic:root_001697:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: P2 51:35 `أَخْرَجْ` (خ ر ج); P2 51:36 `مُسْلِمِينَ` (س ل م); P3 51:40 `أَخَذْ` (ء خ ذ), `نَبَذْ` (ن ب ذ), `يَمِّ` (ي م م).
- Synthesis: The bridge is a reversal in removal's function. P2 takes protected occupants out of a condemned place toward safety; P3 takes the culpable ruler and his force out of their position and casts them into destruction. Judgment sorts by movement, but rescue and punishment assign opposite destinations to the removed object.

### S11. Whole Surah: Death, Shroud, Grave-House, and Estate
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Death releases a person from life, the body is covered and interred in a grave understood as a house, and property remains behind as an estate.
- Active motifs: death expressed as being given rest (`quranic:root_000609:B016/m01`); shrouding and interring the dead (`quranic:root_000266:B009/m01`); grave as burial place (`quranic:root_000266:B009/m02`); grave called a house (`quranic:root_000166:B007/m01`); estate left by the dead (`quranic:root_000180:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:15 `جَنَّٰتٍ` (ج ن ن); 51:36 `بَيْتٍ` (ب ي ت); 51:37 `تَرَكْ` (ت ر ك); 51:39,52 `مَجْنُونٌ` (ج ن ن); 51:41 `رِّيحَ` (ر و ح); 51:56 `جِنَّ` (ج ن ن).
- Synthesis: Release, covering, burial place, and inheritance form one mortuary sequence in which body and possession separate. It usefully pressures the surah's scenes of ruined households and approaching account: a grave may become the body's last terrestrial house and wealth may pass on, but neither operation settles the promised reckoning or erases the trace left for later witnesses.

### S12. Whole Surah: A Right Documented and Restored
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A claimant's right is embodied in a title deed, recognized as the claimant's priority, and returned to its holder.
- Active motifs: deed certifying a right (`quranic:root_000516:B008/m01`); priority and entitlement (`quranic:root_001684:B008/m01`); restoring a right to its owner (`quranic:root_000609:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:39 `تَوَلَّىٰ` (و ل ي); 51:41 `رِّيحَ` (ر و ح); 51:49 `تَذَكَّرُ` (ذ ك ر); 51:54 `تَوَلَّ` (و ل ي); 51:55 `ذَكِّرْ/ذِّكْرَىٰ` (ذ ك ر).
- Synthesis: Record, entitlement, and restitution make a compact legal recovery process. This materializes the right acknowledged in wealth at 51:19 and the coming account at 51:6 as more than voluntary generosity: the share has the force of a documented title whose proper outcome is restoration to the person entitled to it.

### S13. Whole Surah: From Proposal to Wedding House
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A suitor requests marriage, a dowry establishes material obligation, the bride is prepared with a scented face coating, and the groom's entry under a wedding pavilion establishes a new household.
- Active motifs: marriage proposal (`quranic:root_000421:B002/m01`); woman's dowry (`quranic:root_000852:B007/m01`); saffron or perfume coating on a bride's face (`quranic:root_001105:B011/m01`); groom entering his bride under an erected pavilion (`quranic:root_000156:B006/m01`); marriage as establishment of a house (`quranic:root_000166:B010/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:5 `صَادِقٌ` (ص د ق); 51:11 `غَمْرَةٍ` (غ م ر); 51:31 `خَطْبُ` (خ ط ب); 51:36 `بَيْتٍ` (ب ي ت); 51:47 `بَنَيْ` (ب ن ي).
- Synthesis: Request, transfer, bodily preparation, entry, and residence form a complete social transition from two parties to one household. The scene materializes the surah's created pairing and promised child as a social as well as biological order: counterpart relation becomes public commitment, costly obligation, and a house capable of carrying lineage.

### S14. Whole Surah: Date Infusion Prepared and Drunk
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Dates or raisins are dropped into a vessel and covered with water, the drink rests overnight, its surface develops lees or bubbles, and it is taken from a small cup in measured draughts until thirst ends.
- Active motifs: date or raisin infusion made in a water-filled vessel (`quranic:root_001466:B006/m01`); liquid kept or cooled overnight in its container (`quranic:root_000166:B006/m01`); beverage lees or surface bubbles (`quranic:root_001143:B009/m01`); small cup holding little drink (`quranic:root_001105:B009/m01`); draught measured by breaths (`quranic:root_001533:B006/m01`); thirst ended by drinking (`quranic:root_000856:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:11 `غَمْرَةٍ` (غ م ر); 51:21 `أَنفُسِ` (ن ف س); 51:29 `صَرَّةٍ` (ص ر ر); 51:36 `بَيْتٍ` (ب ي ت); 51:40 `نَبَذْ` (ن ب ذ); 51:48 `فَرَشْ` (ف ر ش).
- Synthesis: Ingredient, vessel, waiting time, visible surface change, drinking measure, and bodily outcome make one preparation-and-consumption practice. It materializes hospitality and provision as processes serving embodied thirst, while usefully sharpening the surah's asymmetries of consumption: humans require prepared liquid and repeated draughts, whereas the messengers' untouched meal and the Provider's non-need suspend the ordinary recipient role.

### S15. Whole Surah: Fitting, Honing, and Polishing a Weapon
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A craftsperson seats a point in its shaft, hones iron on stone, finishes the blade's surface, and produces a weapon whose edge and spine retain their hardness.
- Active motifs: fitting a spearpoint into its shaft (`quranic:root_000051:B011/m01`); honing iron, knife, or sword on stone (`quranic:root_001675:B009/m01`); long sharp arrowhead or blade (`quranic:root_000732:B006/m01`); polishing a sword (`quranic:root_000299:B007/m01`); hard keen iron or blade (`quranic:root_000516:B002/m01`); solid exposed spine of sword or arrow (`quranic:root_001396:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:4,44 `أَمْرًا/أَمْرِ` (ء م ر); 51:6 `وَٰقِعٌ` (و ق ع); 51:24 `حَدِيثُ` (ح د ث); 51:38 `سُلْطَٰنٍ` (س ل ط); 51:49,55 `تَذَكَّرُ/ذَكِّرْ/ذِّكْرَىٰ` (ذ ك ر); 51:58 `مَتِينُ` (م ت ن).
- Synthesis: Point, shaft, abrasive, finish, edge, and spine form a bounded manufacture-and-maintenance scene. It usefully pressures the surah's language of authority and strength: a ruler's coercive force depends on fitted parts, abrasive work, and maintained hardness, whereas the Provider's strength and the elemental judgments require no crafted implement to remain effective.

### S16. Whole Surah: A Lie Becomes Public Talk
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A falsehood is fabricated, enters circulation as common talk, is carried between people to provoke discord, attacks a person's reputation, and leaves that person as a story repeated by others.
- Active motifs: fabricated falsehood (`quranic:root_000434:B007/m01`); news renewed through conversation (`quranic:root_000299:B003/m01`); tale-bearing that sows discord (`quranic:root_000043:B009/m01`); backbiting and reputational attack (`quranic:root_001675:B013/m01`); person becoming public story (`quranic:root_000299:B004/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:6 `وَٰقِعٌ` (و ق ع); 51:24 `حَدِيثُ` (ح د ث); 51:27 `تَأْكُلُ` (ء ك ل); 51:49,56 `خَلَقْ` (خ ل ق).
- Synthesis: Fabrication becomes a social transmission chain with maker, circulating utterance, carrier, injured reputation, and durable public outcome. This reframes P1's conflicting speech and P3's inherited “magician or madman” formula as the mechanics of rumor, while the truthful promise and corrective reminder oppose that chain with speech whose recurrence restores reality rather than manufacturing notoriety.

### S17. Whole Surah: Testing a Bow before the Hunt
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hunter inspects the gap between bow and string, draws the string to test tension, sets out, waits until prey comes within reach, and uses the calibrated hunting tool to obtain food.
- Active motifs: bowstring clinging dangerously close to the bow (`quranic:root_000156:B005/m01`); bowstring standing away from the bow's belly (`quranic:root_000170:B008/m01`); testing a bow by drawing its string (`quranic:root_000526:B003/m01`); hunters setting out (`quranic:root_000745:B006/m01`); prey coming within reach (`quranic:root_000421:B005/m01`); bow or trained raptor that feeds its owner (`quranic:root_000934:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 51:7,22-23,47 `سَّمَآءِ/سَّمَآءَ` (س م و); 51:14 `ذُوقُ` (ذ و ق); 51:31 `خَطْبُ` (خ ط ب); 51:38,50-51 `مُّبِينٍ/مُّبِينٌ` (ب ي ن); 51:47 `بَنَيْ` (ب ن ي); 51:57 `يُطْعِمُ` (ط ع م).
- Synthesis: Tool condition, calibration, departure, opportunity, strike, and subsistence outcome form one hunting process. It materializes provision as something a human seeker reaches only through tested tension and favorable timing; the closing declaration of the Provider thereby gains contrast with an agent whose food depends on equipment, pursuit, and prey entering range.
